Estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate >60 >=60 mL/min/1.73 m2 KFH ROSEVILLE LABORATORY

Comment:

Estimated GFR (eGFR) helps us know how the kidneys are working. eGFR is based

on the creatinine blood test result, age, sex, and body size. Monitoring eGFR

over time helps us understand changes in kidney function.

Actual kidney function may be better or worse than the estimated GFR because

blood creatinine levels are affected by body make up, diet, hydration,

genetics, and more.

Multiple eGFR values are used to determine chronic kidney disease (CKD)

stages, defined by the following ranges:

eGFR 45 - 59 = Stage 3a

eGFR 30 - 44 = Stage 3b

eGFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4

eGFR under 15 = Stage 5

Values for eGFR before 05/25/2022 will appear in lab results under "Past

results".

CTA ABDOMINAL AORTA WITH RUNOFF

** HISTORY **:

71 years old, history of right SFA restenosis after prior angioplasty

** TECHNIQUE **:

CT angiogram images of the abdomen, pelvis, and lower extremities acquired with 100 mL Omnipaque 350 intravenous contrast. Maximum intensity projection (MIP) vessel images were obtained.

CTDI: 15.79,8.01 mGy

DLP: 7.89,1057.14 mGy-cm

COMPARISON: CTs 5/31/2022, 1/5/2018

** FINDINGS **:

VASCULAR

HEART: Size is within normal limits.

ABDOMINAL AORTA: Normal abdominal aortic diameter (<3cm). Aortic atherosclerosis. No dissection.

Scattered atherosclerotic calcifications throughout bilateral lower extremity arteries.

RIGHT

Common iliac: Severe stenosis from complex bulky calcification.

External iliac: Mild to moderate stenosis.

Internal iliac: No significant stenosis.

Common femoral artery: Moderate to severe stenosis from bulky posterior wall calcification.

Profunda femoris artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

Superficial Femoral artery: Multifocal moderate to severe stenoses

Popliteal artery: Severe stenosis at proximal segment.

Anterior tibial artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

Tibioperoneal trunk: Patent without significant stenosis.

Posterior tibial artery: Chronically occluded

Peroneal artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

LEFT

Common iliac: Patent without significant stenosis.

External iliac: Mild stenosis.

Internal iliac: No significant stenosis.

Common femoral artery: Mild stenosis.

Profunda femoris artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

Superficial Femoral artery: Multifocal moderate stenoses

Popliteal artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

Anterior tibial artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

Tibioperoneal trunk: Patent without significant stenosis.

Posterior tibial artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

Peroneal artery: Patent without significant stenosis.

NON-VASCULAR

Evaluation of the solid organs is limited due to arterial phase timing of image acquisition.

LUNG BASES: Clear.

LIVER: 0.9 cm round hyperenhancing lesion in the right lobe on series 601 image 60

GALLBLADDER/BILE DUCTS: No calcified gallstones. No significant biliary dilation.

SPLEEN: Normal.

PANCREAS: Normal.

ADRENALS: Normal.

KIDNEYS: Atrophic left kidney. No suspicious renal mass.

GI TRACT: No significant abnormality.

MESENTERY/LYMPH NODES: No suspicious lymph nodes.

PERITONEUM: No significant free fluid. No free air.

BLADDER: No significant abnormality.

PELVIC STRUCTURES: No significant abnormality.

SOFT TISSUES: Small fat-containing umbilical hernia.

BONES: No significant abnormality.

Creatinine 0.79 <=1.11 mg/dL TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate >60 >=60 mL/min/1.73 m2 TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment:

Estimated GFR (eGFR) helps us know how the kidneys are working. eGFR is based

on the creatinine blood test result, age, sex, and body size. Monitoring eGFR

over time helps us understand changes in kidney function.

Actual kidney function may be better or worse than the estimated GFR because

blood creatinine levels are affected by body make up, diet, hydration,

genetics, and more.

Multiple eGFR values are used to determine chronic kidney disease (CKD)

stages, defined by the following ranges:

eGFR 45 - 59 = Stage 3a

eGFR 30 - 44 = Stage 3b

eGFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4

eGFR under 15 = Stage 5

Values for eGFR before 05/25/2022 will appear in lab results under "Past

results".

Comment:

Borderline range of 160 - 199 mg/dL suggests follow-up. Results greater than

or equal to 200 mg/dL is suggestive of diabetes in the setting of classical

symptoms.

Creatinine 0.86 <=1.11 mg/dL KFH WALNUT CREEK LABORATORY

Estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate >60 >=60 mL/min/1.73 m2 KFH WALNUT CREEK LABORATORY

Comment:

Estimated GFR (eGFR) helps us know how the kidneys are working. eGFR is based

on the creatinine blood test result, age, sex, and body size. Monitoring eGFR

over time helps us understand changes in kidney function.

Actual kidney function may be better or worse than the estimated GFR because

blood creatinine levels are affected by body make up, diet, hydration,

genetics, and more.

Multiple eGFR values are used to determine chronic kidney disease (CKD)

stages, defined by the following ranges:

eGFR 45 - 59 = Stage 3a

eGFR 30 - 44 = Stage 3b

eGFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4

eGFR under 15 = Stage 5

Values for eGFR before 05/25/2022 will appear in lab results under "Past

results".

Results - IR ART ATHERECTOMY. (08/23/2022 12:33 PM PDT)

Impressions

08/23/2022 7:22 PM PDT

Angiography of the pelvis and right leg demonstrates multifocal occlusions and stenoses of the superficial femoral artery.

This was successfully treated with directional atherectomy and drug coated balloon angioplasty with excellent angiographic and clinical result. The patient was loaded with Plavix. She is to maintain Plavix treatment for at least 3 months.

Results - IR ART ATHERECTOMY. (08/23/2022 12:33 PM PDT)

Narrative

08/23/2022 7:22 PM PDT

PROCEDURE: RIGHT LEG ARTERIOGRAM

** HISTORY **:

68 years old Female, smoker with recent onset of short distance right leg claudication

** TECHNIQUE **:

PROCEDURE SUMMARY:

  • Arterial access with ultrasound guidance

-Right lower extremity angiography as described below

  • Arterial interventions as described below

  • Post intervention Right lower extremity angiography

Medications: Please see nursing record for sedation medications used.

Other intraprocedural medications: None

Radiation Dose -please see nursing records for radiation dose.

Contrast: Please see nursing record for contrast dose.

** FINDINGS **:

PROCEDURE DETAILS:

Informed consent for the procedure including risks, benefits and alternatives was obtained and time-out was performed prior to the procedure. The right-sided access site was prepared and draped using maximal sterile barrier technique including cutaneous antisepsis.

Local analgesia was achieved with 2% lidocaine administered subcutaneously. The left common femoral artery was accessed with standard micropuncture technique and a sheath was placed. Omniflush catheter was advanced into the distal abdominal aorta and pelvic angiogram was performed.

Over angled glide wire, omniflush catheter was advanced into the right common femoral artery and the lower extremity runoff was performed to the right foot.

Findings:

Severe diffuse atheromatous changes are seen throughout the visualized arterial structures. Extensive calcifications are noted. The vessels as a whole are also quite gracile, especially of the iliac arteries bilaterally.

Both internal iliac arteries are diseased but patent. The right common femoral artery is patent without significant stenosis. The right profunda is disease but is hypertrophied. There is diffuse disease in the SFA with short segment of occlusion in the abductor canal. There is a second longer occlusive segment at distal SFA and a third moderate to high-grade segmental stenosis at the junction of the distal SFA and popliteal arteries. There is two-vessel runoff in the leg with the peroneal artery being the dominant vessel. Anterior tibial is also patent. There is patency of the dorsal pedis as well as the distal posterior tibial artery plantar arteries are patent.

Interventional procedure:

A total of 6000 units of heparin were used for the procedure. In addition 400 mcg of intra-arterial nitroglycerin also were used.

A 6 French destination sheath was advanced over wire so that the distal tip was within the lower right external iliac artery.

An 0.035 advantage wire with crossing catheter was used to successfully traverse the occluded segments.

Then, a 6 mm spider distal protection device was deployed in the distal popliteal artery.

Then, a medium Hawk 1 directional atherectomy device was used to atherectomized all of the mentioned lesions. Approximately 4 passes were made in each segment.

Then 5 mm chocolate balloon was used to dilate all of the affected segments. This was followed up with the use of 5 mm DCB over the treated segments.

Follow-up arteriogram showed excellent angiographic results with complete patency of the SFA established.

The spider distal protection device was retrieved.

Post intervention: Right lower extremity runoff was performed to the foot. There is no evidence for embolic phenomena.

Physical examination revealed a palpable right dorsal pedis artery, whereas prior to the procedure there was none.

Vascular sheath was removed and hemostasis achieved with minx closure device.

Complications: No immediate complications.

Results - IR ART ATHERECTOMY. (08/23/2022 12:33 PM PDT)

Procedure Note

HOWARD YAMAGUCHI YOUNG MD - 08/23/2022

VERTEBRAL ARTERIES: Patent extracranial segments.

Comment:

INTERPRETIVE INFORMATION: Fecal Fat Qualitative

Neutral fats include the monoglycerides, diglycerides, and

triglycerides while split fats are the free fatty acids

that are liberated from them. Impaired synthesis or

secretion of pancreatic enzymes or bile may cause an

increase in neutral fats while an increase in split fats

suggests impaired absorption of nutrients.

D-DIMER (D-DIMER, QUANTITATIVE) - Final result (06/03/2022 9:05 AM PDT)

Results - D-DIMER (D-DIMER, QUANTITATIVE) - Final result (06/03/2022 9:05 AM PDT)

Component Value Ref Range Test Method Analysis Time Performed At Pathologist Signature

D-DIMER 1.27 <=0.49 ug/mL FEU KFH WALNUT CREEK LABORATORY

Comment:

Pulmonary Embolism (PE):

TPMG Chiefs of Emergency Medicine have endorsed the recommendations from the

American College of Physicians and the American College of Cardiology: when

D-dimer testing is applied in the evaluation of pulmonary embolism in patients

without a high pre-test probability and in whom acute PE cannot be excluded

using the PE Rule-Out Criteria (PERC), then age-adjusted D-dimer thresholds

(age x 0.01 ug/mL FEU) should be used in patients over 50 years old instead of

the 0.50 ug/mL FEU threshold to determine the medical necessity of imaging.

Deep Vein Thrombosis (DVT):

D-dimer <0.50 ug/mL FEU in a patient with low pre-test probability makes a

diagnosis of DVT very unlikely. However, a D-dimer >=0.50 ug/mL FEU is

nonspecific and may require additional diagnostic testing.

Disseminated Intravascular Coagulation (DIC):

Use reference interval of <0.50 ug/mL FEU.

Troponin I <0.02 0.00 - 0.04 ng/mL KFH WALNUT CREEK LABORATORY

Comment:

Effective July 15, 2014, NCAL laboratories implemented a new cutoff for

Beckman Tn-I, >0.04ng/mL (>or= 0.05ng/mL flag abnormal in KPHC). The

diagnosis of acute myocardial infarction depends on detection of a rise and/or

fall of cTn or CK-MB with at least one value above the 99th percentile upper

reference limit, >0.04ng/mL, in addition to clinical or ECG findings.

Elevated but unchanging troponin concentrations may be due to myocardial

injury in the absence of acute coronary syndrome.

Creatinine 1.20 <=1.11 mg/dL KFH WALNUT CREEK LABORATORY

Estimated Glomerular Filtration Rate 49 >=60 mL/min/1.73 m2 KFH WALNUT CREEK LABORATORY

Comment:

Estimated GFR (eGFR) helps us know how the kidneys are working. eGFR is based

on the creatinine blood test result, age, sex, and body size. Monitoring eGFR

over time helps us understand changes in kidney function.

Actual kidney function may be better or worse than the estimated GFR because

blood creatinine levels are affected by body make up, diet, hydration,

genetics, and more.

Multiple eGFR values are used to determine chronic kidney disease (CKD)

stages, defined by the following ranges:

eGFR 45 - 59 = Stage 3a

eGFR 30 - 44 = Stage 3b

eGFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4

eGFR under 15 = Stage 5

Values for eGFR before 05/25/2022 will appear in lab results under "Past

results".

reference limit, >0.04ng/mL, in addition to clinical or ECG findings.

injury in the absence of acute coronary syndrome.

Comment:

Estimated GFR (eGFR) helps us know how the kidneys are working. eGFR is based

on the creatinine blood test result, age, sex, and body size. Monitoring eGFR

over time helps us understand changes in kidney function.

Actual kidney function may be better or worse than the estimated GFR because

blood creatinine levels are affected by body make up, diet, hydration,

genetics, and more.

Multiple eGFR values are used to determine chronic kidney disease (CKD)

stages, defined by the following ranges:

eGFR 45 - 59 = Stage 3a

eGFR 30 - 44 = Stage 3b

eGFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4

eGFR under 15 = Stage 5

Values for eGFR before 05/25/2022 will appear in lab results under "Past

results".

D-DIMER (D-DIMER, QUANTITATIVE) - Final result (05/26/2022 12:00 PM PDT)

Results - D-DIMER (D-DIMER, QUANTITATIVE) - Final result (05/26/2022 12:00 PM PDT)

Component Value Ref Range Test Method Analysis Time Performed At Pathologist Signature

D-DIMER 2.39 <=0.49 ug/mL FEU TPMG DUBLIN LABORATORY

Comment:

Pulmonary Embolism (PE):

TPMG Chiefs of Emergency Medicine have endorsed the recommendations from the

American College of Physicians and the American College of Cardiology: when

D-dimer testing is applied in the evaluation of pulmonary embolism in patients

without a high pre-test probability and in whom acute PE cannot be excluded

using the PE Rule-Out Criteria (PERC), then age-adjusted D-dimer thresholds

(age x 0.01 ug/mL FEU) should be used in patients over 50 years old instead of

the 0.50 ug/mL FEU threshold to determine the medical necessity of imaging.

Deep Vein Thrombosis (DVT):

D-dimer <0.50 ug/mL FEU in a patient with low pre-test probability makes a

diagnosis of DVT very unlikely. However, a D-dimer >=0.50 ug/mL FEU is

nonspecific and may require additional diagnostic testing.

Disseminated Intravascular Coagulation (DIC):

Use reference interval of <0.50 ug/mL FEU.

CT HEAD WITHOUT CONTRAST

68 years old, Head trauma, mod-severe

CT images of the head acquired without intravenous contrast.

CTDI: 50.03 mGy

DLP: 804.23 mGy-cm

COMPARISON: MRs 3/29/2015, 12/17/2009, CT 12/1/2009

** FINDINGS **:

BRAIN PARENCHYMA: No acute hemorrhage. No mass effect or herniation. Gray-white differentiation is maintained. Focus of white matter low attenuation abutting the left caudate head corresponding to a finding on the comparison brain MRI, probably small vessel ischemic change.

VENTRICLES/EXTRA-AXIAL SPACES: No hydrocephalus or extra-axial fluid collections.

EXTRACRANIAL STRUCTURES: Normal bones and soft tissues. Visualized paranasal sinuses and mastoids demonstrate no significant abnormality.

03/21/2022 5:02 AM PDT

CT FACIAL BONES WITHOUT CONTRAST

** HISTORY **:

68 years old, Facial trauma

** TECHNIQUE **:

CT images of the facial bones acquired without contrast.

CTDI: 14.19 mGy

DLP: 260.8 mGy-cm

COMPARISON: CT 3/21/2022, MR 3/29/2015

** FINDINGS **:

Mild asymmetric right perioral soft tissue swelling is noted, without displaced fractures of the maxillofacial bones, though artifact from metallic dental hardware partially limits evaluation. Small polypoidal mucosal thickening is noted at the floor of the left maxillary sinus without dependent fluid levels. Nasal septum is convex to the left. Paranasal sinuses, mastoid processes, and middle ear cavities are aerated. Atherosclerotic mural calcification is noted in the intracranial ICAs with 7.7 mm asymmetric prominence of the right cavernous ICA, probably related to known aneurysm with partial calcification. Sclerosis and osteophytes are noted at C1-2 with no displaced fracture of the odontoid process are offset of the lateral masses of C1. Severely reduced disc height is noted at C5-6 and C6-7 with sclerosis and C5 and C6 and prominent ventral osseous spurring at C5-6 and C6-7 and prominent right subarticular disc osteophyte at C5-6 moderately narrowing the right lateral recess with severe right neural foraminal narrowing.

Multilevel arthritic changes are also seen at other levels the partially included upper cervical spine. The temporomandibular joints remain well seated.

Results - CT FACIAL BONES NO CONTRAST. (03/21/2022 4:45 AM PDT)

Creatinine 0.91 <=1.11 mg/dL TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment, glomerular filtration rate SEE NOTE TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment:

Glomerular Filtration Rate (GFR) estimate is expressed in mL/min/1.73 m2 body

surface area. A GFR estimate is validated only for patients greater than or

equal to 18 years of age. Since this test is not

useful if renal function is changing rapidly, a GFR estimate is not reported

for inpatients. Many elderly patients have mildly to moderately reduced GFR.

Per K/DOQI Clinical Practice Guidelines on Chronic Kidney Disease:

GFR 30 - 59 = Stage 3 CKD

GFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4 CKD

GFR < 15 = Stage 5 CKD

Results - CREATININE AND CALCULATED GLOMERULAR FILTRATION RATE (CREATININE W GFR, CALCULATED) - Final result (12/12/2019 12:17 PM PST)

Component Value Ref Range Test Method Analysis Time Performed At Pathologist Signature

Creatinine 0.74 <=1.11 mg/dL TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment, glomerular filtration rate SEE NOTE TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment:

Glomerular Filtration Rate (GFR) estimate is expressed in mL/min/1.73 m2 body

surface area. A GFR estimate is validated only for patients greater than or

equal to 18 years of age. Since this test is not

useful if renal function is changing rapidly, a GFR estimate is not reported

for inpatients. Many elderly patients have mildly to moderately reduced GFR.

Per K/DOQI Clinical Practice Guidelines on Chronic Kidney Disease:

GFR 30 - 59 = Stage 3 CKD

GFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4 CKD

GFR < 15 = Stage 5 CKD

Component Value Ref Range Test Method Analysis Time Performed At Pathologist Signature

Creatinine 0.80 <=1.11 mg/dL TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment, glomerular filtration rate SEE NOTE TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment:

Glomerular Filtration Rate (GFR) estimate is expressed in mL/min/1.73 m2 body

surface area. A GFR estimate is validated only for patients greater than or

equal to 18 years of age. Since this test is not

useful if renal function is changing rapidly, a GFR estimate is not reported

for inpatients. Many elderly patients have mildly to moderately reduced GFR.

Per K/DOQI Clinical Practice Guidelines on Chronic Kidney Disease:

GFR 30 - 59 = Stage 3 CKD

GFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4 CKD

GFR < 15 = Stage 5 CKD

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment, glomerular filtration rate SEE NOTE TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment:

Glomerular Filtration Rate (GFR) estimate is expressed in mL/min/1.73 m2 body

surface area. A GFR estimate is validated only for patients greater than or

equal to 18 years of age. Since this test is not

useful if renal function is changing rapidly, a GFR estimate is not reported

for inpatients. Many elderly patients have mildly to moderately reduced GFR.

Per K/DOQI Clinical Practice Guidelines on Chronic Kidney Disease:

GFR 30 - 59 = Stage 3 CKD

GFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4 CKD

GFR < 15 = Stage 5 CKD

Creatinine 0.77 <=1.11 mg/dL TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

--- >60 >=60 mL/min TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment, glomerular filtration rate SEE NOTE TPMG REGIONAL LABORATORY, MWS

Comment:

Glomerular Filtration Rate (GFR) estimate is expressed in mL/min/1.73 m2 body

surface area. A GFR estimate is validated only for patients greater than or

equal to 18 years of age. Since this test is not

useful if renal function is changing rapidly, a GFR estimate is not reported

for inpatients. Many elderly patients have mildly to moderately reduced GFR.

Per K/DOQI Clinical Practice Guidelines on Chronic Kidney Disease:

GFR 30 - 59 = Stage 3 CKD

GFR 15 - 29 = Stage 4 CKD

GFR < 15 = Stage 5 CKD
